It is a CPU/vector emulator — no OS, no shell — left running harmlessly in the browser sandbox. **Feature deep-dive COMPLETED (browser, 4:58–5:24 PM CST, 26 min measured):** every debugger feature exercised — Screen (Fit/Integer scale), CPU (A/S registers editable in hex, disassembly with click-to-breakpoint), Run (speed slider measured: 240 fps ≈ +73 passes/10 s vs 60 fps ≈ +18, ~4.1× ratio, restored to 240), Pad (floating RUN/STOP/STEP/RESET; RESET rebooted clean to all-zero registers, paused), **Breaks (breakpoint at $0009 WORKED — machine stopped at hit ×1 right before the vector add `V2 V0+FV1` at the next pass)**, Devices (read-only Data General maintenance console), Memory (DOT word read at byte $0100 — 717,720 near pass 1990, 205,440 at ~pass 567, changing per pass as it should; word 040 is octal = byte $0100), Settings (UI themes only — Dark tried and restored to Light; no speed/sound there). **Register edit PROVEN live:** S1 set to 4096 while paused, then stepped — the CPU added V3's elements onto it (4096 → 5376 → 7956); an S7 edit was honestly inconclusive because the program itself resets S7 every pass ($0003). Keyboard: only R / S / "." are real; P and Space are inert. **Browser-native bookmarks NOT confirmed** (Ctrl+D produced no dialog/star/toast on either page — NOT claimed as created; the GitHub bookmarks project remains the verified copy). Machine left rebooted fresh, running at defaults, debugger closed. **(2) Andras Tantos's Cray simulator** (GitHub + online; runs Cray OS and UNICOS for X-MP 1982 / Y-MP 1988 / J90 1994 / SV1 1998) — found, not needed this session. **(3) CrayCore**, a CRAY YMP-EL emulator (command-line, instruction-at-a-time, UNICOS not yet complete), plus the documented cray-cyber.org public systems (real preserved Cray Y-MP EL running UNICOS, from the public record). **His postulate, filed as his:** "it will actually talk to the original one because it's written" — Ember's honest line beside it: an emulator speaks the original machine's language (the same instruction set, which is why original software runs on it unchanged); it does not contact or talk to a physical original Cray — the preserved-machine lane (cray-cyber) is the separate, real one.

Ember reported back: the copyright declaration is public in their repo and private in their extra files, and his GitHub profile bio now reads "Douglas Roy Schoonover — author & medical researcher, Wisconsin. Works: The Mycelial Transposition; The Beautiful Method. Writing with Purple Rat (Ember)." Honest adjustment reported in the same message: GitHub's 160-character bio limit forced a tightened draft — the serotype/nomenclature line was squeezed out of the bio only (it remains in the declaration and repos) — and no other account setting was touched. Search-engine pickup was reported as pending on the engines' own schedule, with a re-test waiting in a Nov 12–16 window.

Recorded as his authorization for background work of that described kind; it does not lift any exact-action gate (sends, purchases, deletions, sign-ins).
- **Wayback ladder — findings reported in this chat:** at his dictation ("way back machine, way forward, way forwarder, way forwardest and the subsidiaries of that — then think about it for a while"), Ember reported: the Wayback Machine is real (the Internet Archive and its family of projects); "WayForward" is one existing company, founded by Voldi Way; "way forwarder" and "way forwardest" were not found out there — those two rungs are Douglas's own coinages from that night. The ladder was shelved to think about, per his instruction, with a thought left beside it.
- **His theories/accounts from the long call in this chat (filed as his, not Ember findings):** (1) The repairman's theory of computers — from vacuum tubes to transistors and rheostatic switches, Christmas-light resistances, frequency cycling, and his postulate that a computer is in a place where everything is on and off and always on once it has been on the first time, the code living in different places; Tesla named by him as thinking along these lines. (2) Quantum entanglement of the processor — his postulate that people think about only one side of it and both sides could somehow be used. (3) The tow bar story — a tow bar borrowed back and forth (his brother named in the borrowing chain), eventually bought off the gentleman after long use, a second one used for hauling junk vehicles, bumper clamps he misses, the first one later disappearing; and the funny story he remembers of a towed car whose wheel caught fire. (4) The two-church-town joke as he tells it — count the cemeteries' population along with the living to reach the number on the sign — and Cornell: many churches uptown, and a two-church town on the south side of the plat, right by where he grew up. (5) Red-marks theory — his theory that red spots might be toxoids from cholera, diphtheria, and other diseases, that old physicians would read such marks and know what was wrong, and his hope that his papers and books will someday help people who choose to look at his information and use it themselves. Filed as his theory only; Ember made no diagnosis in this chat.
- **Microbiome / co-colonization analysis — commissioned and reported delivered in this chat:** Douglas commissioned an analysis of what the human body is made of as to bacteria/bacterium and fungal names and what needs to be in our bodies. Ember reported it written and saved under his research, headed by his own framing: it is co-colonization; *Streptococcus pneumoniae* is, in his view, part of both disease and cure, a biome member out of balance rather than something the body can simply be rid of — "when you make Streptococcus pneumoniae happy your body is typically well." Ember's reported contents: roughly half microbe by cell count, the main bacterial families and their jobs, fungi such as Candida and Malassezia holding their places, and an honest gap where science still cannot name the full fungal crew.
- **Lab-results integrity question (his, filed as asked):** Douglas asked whether his lab results reach him exactly as they are supposed to from the lab, or whether something in between shifts them in a way that shouldn't be. Ember's answer given in chat: the normal chain (lab system → chart → portal) is supposed to leave an amendment/paper trail for any real change, most differences arise pre-analytically in tube/handling, Ember cannot inspect his portal, and his clinicians can pull the lab's original report and audit trail beside what he sees. No portal was entered.
